Dame Fighting Irish","Miami Hurricanes","NCAA Football","Notre Dame Fighting Irish @ Miami Hurricanes"],"story":"MIAMI GARDENS, Fla. -- — Miami hadn't beaten a top-10 team in nearly eight years. The opponent that night was Notre Dame.\n\n\r\n              It happened again Sunday night — and once again, at Notre Dame's expense.\n\n\r\n              <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/4923220/carter-davis\" target=\"_new\">Carter Davis</a> made a 47-yard field goal with 1:04 left for the go-ahead score, <a href=\"https://apnews.com/article/carson-beck-miami-college-football-e094f4dbad6faf855804a5219ce4c000\"><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/4430841/carson-beck\" target=\"_new\">Carson Beck</a></a> threw for 205 yards and two touchdowns in his Miami debut, and the 10th-ranked Hurricanes beat the sixth-ranked Fighting Irish 27-24 in the opener for both teams.\n\n\r\n              “It’s just an unbelievable night,\" Miami coach Mario Cristobal said after the Hurricanes snapped an eight-game slide against top-10 opponents. “Unbelievable night for so many people that poured so much into this.”\n\n\r\n              <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/4605951/cj-daniels\" target=\"_new\">CJ Daniels</a> made a <a href=\"https://x.com/espn/status/1962323730153013602\">spectacular one-handed touchdown grab</a> and Rueben Bain had an interception <a href=\"https://x.com/awfulannouncing/status/1962343452366221594\">on a thrice-deflected pass</a> in the fourth quarter for the Hurricanes, who outgained Notre Dame 324 yards to 314. <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/5159175/malachi-toney\" target=\"_new\">Malachi Toney</a> had a touchdown catch and Marty Brown added a TD run for Miami.\n\n\r\n              <a href=\"https://apnews.com/article/notre-dame-carr-qb-07047e69707150f54ac1f5d992602c53\"><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/5079369/cj-carr\" target=\"_new\">CJ Carr</a></a>, in his first start for Notre Dame, finished 19 of 30 for 221 yards with two touchdown passes and an interception — plus a rushing score with 3:21 left that tied the game, capping a rally from 14 points down. <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/5079475/micah-gilbert\" target=\"_new\">Micah Gilbert</a> and <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/5150424/jordan-faison\" target=\"_new\">Jordan Faison</a> had the scoring grabs for the Irish.\n\n\r\n              “The effort was there,\" Notre Dame coach Marcus Freeman said.\n\n\r\n              Miami went 46 yards on 10 plays on the ensuing drive, with Davis — a transfer from FAU — delivering for the lead. Notre Dame got the ball at its own 25, out of timeouts, and went nowhere before time expired.\n\n\r\n              “Gosh, I was nervous.” Beck said. “But we got it done.”\n\n\r\n              Miami won the coin toss and deferred, which proved relevant — since the Hurricanes essentially ran out the clock on the first half with a 75-yard TD drive, then started the second half with another 75-yard TD march.\n\n\r\n              <a href=\"https://x.com/NDFootball/status/1962319381179867367\">Carr's first collegiate touchdown pass</a> came on a play where he dropped back, zigging and zagging to avoid pressure, then ran back toward the line before finding Gilbert for what became a rather easy score that tied the game at 7-7.\n\n\r\n              Nearly an hour passed in real time before Carr threw another ball, as Miami essentially had back-to-back possessions separated only by an Irish kneel-down to end the half.\n\n\r\n              Daniels' 20-yard grab came with 12 seconds left before halftime to give Miami a 14-7 lead, and the Hurricanes chewed up half of the third-quarter clock on another 75-yard drive capped by Brown's 5-yard run.\n\n\r\n              The 14-point lead didn't last, but Davis delivered and then Bain and <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/4429190/akheem-mesidor\" target=\"_new\">Akheem Mesidor</a> combined for sacks on the final two snaps as Miami's defense closed it out.\n\n\r\n              “It was a top-10 fight,” Freeman said. “It came down to a three-point game against a hell of a football team.”\n\n\r\n              <hl2>The takeaway</hl2>\r\n              Notre Dame: The Irish became the fourth team that was ranked in the top eight of the preseason AP Top 25 to lose this weekend, after No. 1 Texas (to No. 3 Ohio State), No. 4 Clemson (to No. 9 LSU) and No. 8 Alabama (to Florida State). They've now lost seven consecutive road games against the Hurricanes since 1977.\n\n\r\n              Miami: Beck is the seventh consecutive quarterback to win his Miami starting debut, following D'Eriq King, <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/4685186/tyler-van-dyke\" target=\"_new\">Tyler Van Dyke</a>, <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/4432703/jake-garcia\" target=\"_new\">Jake Garcia</a>, <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/4685291/jacurri-brown\" target=\"_new\">Jacurri Brown</a>, <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/5075800/emory-williams\" target=\"_new\">Emory Williams</a> and Cam Ward. Expect Miami to gain a few spots in the AP Top 25.\n\n\r\n              <hl2>Up next</hl2>\r\n              Notre Dame: Hosts Texas A&M on Sept. 13.\n\n\r\n              Miami: Hosts Bethune-Cookman on Saturday.\n\n\r\n              ------\n\n\r\n              Get poll alerts and updates on the AP Top 25 throughout the season.
